The company has over 50 years of history in community-based healthcare technology. Netsmart is currently owned by private equity firms GI Partners and TA Associates (since 2018). Prior ownership history includes Genstar Capital (2010–2016) and a joint venture with Allscripts (2016–2018).\n     9→\n    10→Netsmart's primary markets are behavioral health, human services, post-acute care (home health, hospice, skilled nursing), and senior living. Their main EHR platform is called CareFabric, which encompasses several distinct products: myAvatar (behavioral health), myEvolv (human services/addiction treatment), myUnity (post-acute care), GEHRIMED (geriatric medicine), and TheraOffice (physical therapy/rehabilitation). They also offer myHealthPointe (patient engagement), myInsight (public health), and CarePathways (quality measures).\n    11→\n    12→Netsmart acquired TheraOffice in April 2022, adding outpatient physical therapy and rehabilitation to their portfolio. TheraOffice was originally founded in 2001 by Dan Morrill (a practicing physical therapist) and Ryan Havlick (a software developer) and operated independently under the company name Hands On Technology, Inc. before the acquisition.\n    13→\n    14→## Product: TheraOffice\n    15→\n    16→CHPL ID: 11493\n    17→\n    18→### What It Is\n    19→\n    20→TheraOffice is a specialty EMR and practice management platform designed specifically for outpatient physical therapy, occupational therapy, and speech-language pathology (SLP) practices. It is an all-in-one system combining clinical documentation, scheduling, billing/revenue cycle management, and reporting in a single integrated platform. The certified module (TheraOffice v14.1) appears to be the full product — not a component of something larger, though it now integrates with Netsmart's broader CareFabric ecosystem.\n    21→\n    22→The product is cloud-hosted (available as both on-site and web-based historically, but now primarily cloud-based) and HIPAA-compliant. It was ONC-certified (Cures Update) on June 28, 2024, by Drummond Group, with a broad set of criteria including clinical data (a)(1),(a)(5),(a)(12),(a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(3), patient portal (e)(1),(e)(3), clinical quality measures (c)(1)–(c)(3), public health reporting (f)(1),(f)(5), FHIR API (g)(10), and direct messaging (h)(1).\n    23→\n    24→### Users & Market\n    25→\n    26→TheraOffice serves outpatient rehabilitation practices — primarily small to mid-size physical therapy clinics, but also occupational therapy and speech-language pathology practices. The product was named #1 EMR and Practice Management solution for physical therapy and outpatient rehabilitation in the 2025 Black Book Research Survey, with 902 rehabilitation/PT practices evaluating 58 competing products.\n    27→\n    28→Netsmart reports TheraOffice is used by over 900 practices. The typical users are physical therapists, occupational therapists, speech-language pathologists, billing staff, and practice managers. It supports both pediatric and adult outpatient rehabilitation, as well as home health rehabilitation (Medicare Part B) and specialty rehabilitation services including pelvic health, vestibular, low vision, and lymphedema.\n    29→\n    30→The product targets private therapy practices and multi-site rehab groups. It serves both solo practitioners and multi-location operations, with centralized administrative monitoring across sites.\n    31→\n    32→### Modules & Functionality\n    33→\n    34→Based on vendor materials, press releases, and third-party reviews, TheraOffice has the following core modules and features:\n    35→\n    36→**Clinical Documentation**\n    37→- Over 40 customizable templates covering PT, OT, SLP, McKenzie, lymphedema, pelvic health, pediatric, and vestibular specialties\n    38→- Claims to reduce documentation time by up to 80% — daily notes in 1–2 minutes, initial evaluations in 5–7 minutes\n    39→- Goal creation tools and treatment planning\n    40→- Condition/pain history tracking\n    41→- AI documentation assistant with speech-to-text and noise filtering for hands-free charting\n    42→- MIPS (Merit-Based Incentive Payment System) documentation and quality measure support — TheraOffice operates as a Qualified Registry for Medicare compliance\n    43→\n    44→**Scheduling**\n    45→- Appointment scheduling with recurring appointment support\n    46→- Mobile check-in and intake validation\n    47→- Co-pay collection at check-in\n    48→- Schedule Optimizer tool to automatically fill cancellations from waitlists\n    49→- Online patient scheduling (patient-facing appointment booking)\n    50→\n    51→**Billing & Revenue Cycle Management**\n    52→- Fully integrated billing connected to scheduling and documentation\n    53→- Claims processing (submission, tracking, denial management)\n    54→- ERA (Electronic Remittance Advice) downloads\n    55→- Denial tracking and aging reports\n    56→- Payment posting automation\n    57→- Collections management\n    58→- Telemedicine billing\n    59→- TheraOffice Pay: integrated card processing, tap-to-pay, mobile payments (Apple/Google Pay), chip reader support\n    60→- Ability to save patient card profiles for recurring payments\n    61→\n    62→**Patient Portal**\n    63→- Digital patient intake (eliminates paper forms)\n    64→- Prescription requests and refill checking\n    65→- Appointment booking\n    66→- Online payments\n    67→- Patient-provider communication/messaging\n    68→- Access to health records (HIPAA-compliant)\n    69→\n    70→**E-Prescribing**\n    71→- Electronic prescription and refill capabilities\n    72→- Analysis of patient reports, treatment history, and insurance coverage for prescription generation\n    73→- Authorized refills for controlled substances (EPCS)\n    74→\n    75→**Telehealth**\n    76→- Built-in virtual visit functionality integrated with scheduling, documentation, and billing\n    77→\n    78→**Reporting & Analytics**\n    79→- 175+ built-in reports\n    80→- KPIs and productivity tracking\n    81→- HSA receipts\n    82→- Sign-in logs\n    83→- Visit history reports\n    84→- Custom report capabilities\n    85→\n    86→**Communication & Document Management**\n    87→- Inbound/outbound e-faxing within the platform\n    88→- Automated appointment reminders\n    89→- Electronic referral management\n    90→\n    91→**Integrations**\n    92→- Integration with Netsmart CareFabric ecosystem\n    93→- Third-party integrations including QuickBooks and Google Calendar mentioned by a review site (FindEMR)\n    94→- The ONC certification press release references \"near seamless and secure exchange of patient health data and electronic referrals among different healthcare providers\"\n    95→- Direct messaging (h)(1) certification implies support for Direct protocol for health information exchange\n    96→- FHIR API (g)(10) certification for standardized data access\n    97→\n    98→**Compliance & Security**\n    99→- Automatic backups and redundant data storage\n   100→- Real-time traffic re-routing during outages\n   101→- Password misuse monitoring\n   102→- Multi-factor authentication\n   103→- Multi-level daily risk assessments\n   104→- 99.9% uptime claimed\n   105→\n   106→### Data & Content\n   107→\n   108→Based on the features and certifications described above, TheraOffice stores and manages the following categories of data:\n   109→\n   110→- **Patient demographics**: Names, contact details, insurance information, patient registration data\n   111→- **Clinical documentation**: Evaluations, progress notes, daily notes, treatment plans, goals, condition/pain history — all via customizable templates across therapy specialties\n   112→- **Scheduling data**: Appointments, recurring schedules, cancellations, waitlists, check-in records\n   113→- **Billing and financial data**: Claims, ERA/remittance data, denial records, aging reports, payment transactions, patient card profiles, co-pay records, collections data, HSA receipt data\n   114→- **Prescriptions**: E-prescribing data including controlled substance prescriptions (EPCS), refill history\n   115→- **Patient portal data**: Patient messages, intake form submissions, appointment requests, online payment records\n   116→- **Telehealth records**: Virtual visit records (integrated with documentation and billing)\n   117→- **Fax/document management**: Inbound and outbound fax documents, referral documents\n   118→- **Quality measures**: MIPS/quality reporting data, clinical quality measure calculations\n   119→- **Outcome tracking**: Treatment effectiveness measures, care plan adjustments\n   120→- **Audit data**: Sign-in logs, user access records, audit events (certified for (d) criteria)\n   121→- **Public health reporting data**: Immunization data ((f)(1) certified), electronic case reporting ((f)(5) certified)\n   122→\n   123→**Notable**: The certified criteria include (a)(5) Demographics, (a)(1) CPOE, (a)(12) Family health history, and (a)(14) Implantable device list — somewhat surprising for a PT-focused product, but these are required for ONC certification breadth. The (a)(1) CPOE certification implies the product supports some form of computerized provider order entry (likely for diagnostic tests, imaging referrals, or medication orders within the therapy context). The (a)(12) family health history criterion suggests the product stores family health history data. The (a)(14) implantable device list means the product can track patient implantable devices.\n   124→\n   125→**Gaps in research**: The vendor website does not provide detailed technical documentation about the data model or database schema. The specific structure of how clinical notes, billing data, and patient records are stored internally is not publicly documented. Integration capabilities beyond what's listed are unclear — the vendor mentions \"multiple other Netsmart solutions\" but doesn't enumerate specific data exchange pathways. Whether TheraOffice stores lab results or imaging data directly (vs. receiving them via interfaces) is unclear from the marketing materials, though the (b)(1)–(b)(3) transitions of care certifications suggest it can receive and display C-CDA clinical summaries from other systems.\n   126→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. The company has over 50 years of history in community-based healthcare technology. Netsmart is currently owned by private equity firms GI Partners and TA Associates (since 2018). Prior ownership history includes Genstar Capital (2010–2016) and a joint venture with Allscripts (2016–2018).\n\nNetsmart's primary markets are behavioral health, human services, post-acute care (home health, hospice, skilled nursing), and senior living. Their main EHR platform is called CareFabric, which encompasses several distinct products: myAvatar (behavioral health), myEvolv (human services/addiction treatment), myUnity (post-acute care), GEHRIMED (geriatric medicine), and TheraOffice (physical therapy/rehabilitation). They also offer myHealthPointe (patient engagement), myInsight (public health), and CarePathways (quality measures).\n\nNetsmart acquired TheraOffice in April 2022, adding outpatient physical therapy and rehabilitation to their portfolio. TheraOffice was originally founded in 2001 by Dan Morrill (a practicing physical therapist) and Ryan Havlick (a software developer) and operated independently under the company name Hands On Technology, Inc. before the acquisition.\n\n## Product: TheraOffice\n\nCHPL ID: 11493\n\n### What It Is\n\nTheraOffice is a specialty EMR and practice management platform designed specifically for outpatient physical therapy, occupational therapy, and speech-language pathology (SLP) practices. It is an all-in-one system combining clinical documentation, scheduling, billing/revenue cycle management, and reporting in a single integrated platform. The certified module (TheraOffice v14.1) appears to be the full product — not a component of something larger, though it now integrates with Netsmart's broader CareFabric ecosystem.\n\nThe product is cloud-hosted (available as both on-site and web-based historically, but now primarily cloud-based) and HIPAA-compliant. It was ONC-certified (Cures Update) on June 28, 2024, by Drummond Group, with a broad set of criteria including clinical data (a)(1),(a)(5),(a)(12),(a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(3), patient portal (e)(1),(e)(3), clinical quality measures (c)(1)–(c)(3), public health reporting (f)(1),(f)(5), FHIR API (g)(10), and direct messaging (h)(1).\n\n### Users & Market\n\nTheraOffice serves outpatient rehabilitation practices — primarily small to mid-size physical therapy clinics, but also occupational therapy and speech-language pathology practices. The product was named #1 EMR and Practice Management solution for physical therapy and outpatient rehabilitation in the 2025 Black Book Research Survey, with 902 rehabilitation/PT practices evaluating 58 competing products.\n\nNetsmart reports TheraOffice is used by over 900 practices. The typical users are physical therapists, occupational therapists, speech-language pathologists, billing staff, and practice managers. It supports both pediatric and adult outpatient rehabilitation, as well as home health rehabilitation (Medicare Part B) and specialty rehabilitation services including pelvic health, vestibular, low vision, and lymphedema.\n\nThe product targets private therapy practices and multi-site rehab groups. It serves both solo practitioners and multi-location operations, with centralized administrative monitoring across sites.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor materials, press releases, and third-party reviews, TheraOffice has the following core modules and features:\n\n**Clinical Documentation**\n- Over 40 customizable templates covering PT, OT, SLP, McKenzie, lymphedema, pelvic health, pediatric, and vestibular specialties\n- Claims to reduce documentation time by up to 80% — daily notes in 1–2 minutes, initial evaluations in 5–7 minutes\n- Goal creation tools and treatment planning\n- Condition/pain history tracking\n- AI documentation assistant with speech-to-text and noise filtering for hands-free charting\n- MIPS (Merit-Based Incentive Payment System) documentation and quality measure support — TheraOffice operates as a Qualified Registry for Medicare compliance\n\n**Scheduling**\n- Appointment scheduling with recurring appointment support\n- Mobile check-in and intake validation\n- Co-pay collection at check-in\n- Schedule Optimizer tool to automatically fill cancellations from waitlists\n- Online patient scheduling (patient-facing appointment booking)\n\n**Billing & Revenue Cycle Management**\n- Fully integrated billing connected to scheduling and documentation\n- Claims processing (submission, tracking, denial management)\n- ERA (Electronic Remittance Advice) downloads\n- Denial tracking and aging reports\n- Payment posting automation\n- Collections management\n- Telemedicine billing\n- TheraOffice Pay: integrated card processing, tap-to-pay, mobile payments (Apple/Google Pay), chip reader support\n- Ability to save patient card profiles for recurring payments\n\n**Patient Portal**\n- Digital patient intake (eliminates paper forms)\n- Prescription requests and refill checking\n- Appointment booking\n- Online payments\n- Patient-provider communication/messaging\n- Access to health records (HIPAA-compliant)\n\n**E-Prescribing**\n- Electronic prescription and refill capabilities\n- Analysis of patient reports, treatment history, and insurance coverage for prescription generation\n- Authorized refills for controlled substances (EPCS)\n\n**Telehealth**\n- Built-in virtual visit functionality integrated with scheduling, documentation, and billing\n\n**Reporting & Analytics**\n- 175+ built-in reports\n- KPIs and productivity tracking\n- HSA receipts\n- Sign-in logs\n- Visit history reports\n- Custom report capabilities\n\n**Communication & Document Management**\n- Inbound/outbound e-faxing within the platform\n- Automated appointment reminders\n- Electronic referral management\n\n**Integrations**\n- Integration with Netsmart CareFabric ecosystem\n- Third-party integrations including QuickBooks and Google Calendar mentioned by a review site (FindEMR)\n- The ONC certification press release references \"near seamless and secure exchange of patient health data and electronic referrals among different healthcare providers\"\n- Direct messaging (h)(1) certification implies support for Direct protocol for health information exchange\n- FHIR API (g)(10) certification for standardized data access\n\n**Compliance & Security**\n- Automatic backups and redundant data storage\n- Real-time traffic re-routing during outages\n- Password misuse monitoring\n- Multi-factor authentication\n- Multi-level daily risk assessments\n- 99.9% uptime claimed\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the features and certifications described above, TheraOffice stores and manages the following categories of data:\n\n- **Patient demographics**: Names, contact details, insurance information, patient registration data\n- **Clinical documentation**: Evaluations, progress notes, daily notes, treatment plans, goals, condition/pain history — all via customizable templates across therapy specialties\n- **Scheduling data**: Appointments, recurring schedules, cancellations, waitlists, check-in records\n- **Billing and financial data**: Claims, ERA/remittance data, denial records, aging reports, payment transactions, patient card profiles, co-pay records, collections data, HSA receipt data\n- **Prescriptions**: E-prescribing data including controlled substance prescriptions (EPCS), refill history\n- **Patient portal data**: Patient messages, intake form submissions, appointment requests, online payment records\n- **Telehealth records**: Virtual visit records (integrated with documentation and billing)\n- **Fax/document management**: Inbound and outbound fax documents, referral documents\n- **Quality measures**: MIPS/quality reporting data, clinical quality measure calculations\n- **Outcome tracking**: Treatment effectiveness measures, care plan adjustments\n- **Audit data**: Sign-in logs, user access records, audit events (certified for (d) criteria)\n- **Public health reporting data**: Immunization data ((f)(1) certified), electronic {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01W4Pb7F1BbPd3obYNpukzQk","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Hy1MdkDTgdWWpfvBgfk45j","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/results/netsmart-technologies--theraoffice/downloads/enrichment/extract-tables.ts","content":"#!/usr/bin/env bun\n/**\n * Extract table and column definitions from the TheraOffice EHI Export PDF.\n *\n * Usage:\n *   bun run extract-tables.ts\n *\n * Input:  ../ehi_tables/EHI Export All Tables - May 2024 (TheraOffice).pdf\n *         (via pdftotext)\n * Output: tables-catalog.json   — structured table/column definitions\n *         coverage-accounting.json — parse statistics\n */\n\nimport { $ } from \"bun\";\n\nconst PDF_PATH = new URL(\n  \"../ehi_tables/EHI Export All Tables - May 2024 (TheraOffice).pdf\",\n  import.meta.url\n).pathname;\n\nconst OUT_DIR = new URL(\".\", import.meta.url).pathname;\n\n// Extract text from PDF using pdftotext\nconst raw = await $`pdftotext ${PDF_PATH} -`.text();\n\ninterface Column {\n  name: string;\n  data_type: string;\n  max_length: number;\n}\n\ninterface Table {\n  name: string;\n  columns: Column[];\n  total_columns: number;\n}\n\n// Parse the PDF text into table structures.\n// The format is:\n//   TABLE_NAME\n//   Columns\n//   Name    Data type    Max length\n//   COL1    type1        len1\n//   ...\n//   Total: N column(s)\n\nconst lines = raw.split(\"\\n\");\nconst tables: Table[] = [];\nlet currentTable: Table | null = null;\nlet inColumns = false;\nlet headerSeen = false;\n\n// Known table names from the TOC\nconst knownTables = new Set([\n  \"PAT_PROFILE\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_CORE\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_CORE_ETHNICITY\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_CORE_GENDER\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_CORE_RACE\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_ALLERGIES\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_FAMILY_HISTORY\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_FAMILY_MEMBER\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_GOALS\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_IMMUNIZATIONS\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_IMMUNIZATIONS_ACKNOWLEDGEMENT\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_IMPLANTDEVS\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_LABS\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_MEDICATIONS\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_PROBLEMS\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_PROCEDURES\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_PROGRAM_ADMISSION\",\n  \"PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_VITALSIGNS\",\n  \"PTCASE\",\n]);\n\n// SQL Server data types we expect\nconst sqlTypes = new Set([\n  \"int\",\n  \"varchar\",\n  \"smalldatetime\",\n  \"datetime\",\n  \"tinyint\",\n  \"smallint\",\n  \"bit\",\n  \"decimal\",\n  \"char\",\n  \"uniqueidentifier\",\n]);\n\nfor (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {\n  const line = lines[i].trim();\n\n  // Skip empty lines and copyright footers\n  if (!line || line.startsWith(\"Confidential.\") || /^\\d+$/.test(line)) continue;\n\n  // Detect table name\n  if (knownTables.has(line)) {\n    if (currentTable) {\n      tables.push(currentTable);\n    }\n    currentTable = { name: line, columns: [], total_columns: 0 };\n    inColumns = false;\n    headerSeen = false;\n    continue;\n  }\n\n  // Detect start of columns section\n  if (line === \"Columns\" && currentTable) {\n    inColumns = true;\n    continue;\n  }\n\n  // Skip the header row\n  if (inColumns && !headerSeen && line.startsWith(\"Name\")) {\n    headerSeen = true;\n    continue;\n  }\n\n  // Detect total line\n  const totalMatch = line.match(/^Total:\\s+(\\d+)\\s+column/);\n  if (totalMatch && currentTable) {\n    currentTable.total_columns = parseInt(totalMatch[1]);\n    inColumns = false;\n    continue;\n  }\n\n  // Parse column definitions. In the extracted text, each column's fields\n  // appear on separate lines: NAME, then data_type, then max_length.\n  // We handle both same-line (tab/space-separated) and multi-line formats.\n  if (inColumns && headerSeen && currentTable) {\n    // Check if this is a data type on its own line (follows a column name)\n    if (sqlTypes.has(line)) {\n      // This is a data type for the last column that doesn't have one yet\n      const lastCol = currentTable.columns[currentTable.columns.length - 1];\n      if (lastCol && !lastCol.data_type) {\n        lastCol.data_type = line;\n      }\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    // Check if this is a max_length on its own line\n    if (/^\\d+$/.test(line)) {\n      const lastCol = currentTable.columns[currentTable.columns.length - 1];\n      if (lastCol && lastCol.max_length === 0) {\n        lastCol.max_length = parseInt(line);\n      }\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    // Check for very large max_length values (like 2147483647)\n    if (/^\\d{5,}$/.test(line)) {\n      const lastCol = currentTable.columns[currentTable.columns.length - 1];\n      if (lastCol && lastCol.max_length === 0) {\n        lastCol.max_length = parseInt(line);\n      }\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    // This should be a column name (all-caps identifier)\n    if (/^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]+$/.test(line)) {\n      currentTable.columns.push({\n        name: line,\n        data_type: \"\",\n        max_length: 0,\n      });\n      continue;\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// Push the last table\nif (currentTable) {\n  tables.push(currentTable);\n}\n\n// Build the catalog\nconst catalog = {\n  source: \"EHI Export All Tables - ${f}`);\n  }\n}\nif (missingDataType.length > 0) {\n  console.log(`Columns missing data_type: ${missingDataType.length}`);\n}\nconsole.log(\"Done. For TheraOffice, the documentation consists of a single PDF titled \"EHI Export: TheraOffice Form and Table Documentation\" (last updated May 29, 2024).\n\nThe documentation describes a **delimited file export** — a manual, one-time export of health data for one or more patients. Each database table is exported to its own file with a filename matching the table name. The format is described as \"computable, delimited file format native to TheraOffice.\"\n\nThe PDF provides:\n- A brief overview explaining the export mechanism\n- A glossary of common fields (Id, Pat_ID, MODIFIED_USER, MODIFIED_DATE, CREATED_USER, CREATED_DATE)\n- Table-by-table documentation with column names, SQL Server data types, and max lengths\n\nThe documentation also notes that \"some electronic health information might not be available in a table format, such as rich text documents or images. sex, disabilities, smoking status, previous address) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_CORE_ETHNICITY | 3 | Patient ethnicity (coded) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_CORE_GENDER | 3 | Patient gender identity (coded) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_CORE_RACE | 3 | Patient race (coded) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_ALLERGIES | 18 | Allergies (name, type, reaction, severity, status, SNOMED/RxNorm/MED-RT codes) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_FAMILY_HISTORY | 13 | Family health history conditions (SNOMED/ICD-10 coded) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_FAMILY_MEMBER | 30 | Family member details (contact info, relationship, conditions) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_GOALS | 12 | Patient goals (description, due date, status) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_IMMUNIZATIONS | 52 | Immunization records (vaccine, administration details, lot, guardian info) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_IMMUNIZATIONS_ACKNOWLEDGEMENT | 3 | Immunization acknowledgement text |\n| PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_IMPLANTDEVS | 22 | Implantable devices (UDI, serial, manufacturer, MRI safety) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_LABS | 94 | Laboratory results (comprehensive: order info, specimen, facility, provider, results) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_MEDICATIONS | 18 | Medications (name, RxNorm, dosage, frequency, route, provider) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_PROBLEMS | 12 | Problem list (SNOMED/ICD-10 coded conditions) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_PROCEDURES | 13 | Procedures (code, name, date, notes) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_PROGRAM_ADMISSION | 11 | Program admissions (admission/discharge dates, disposition, reason, type codes) |\n| PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_VITALSIGNS | 26 | Vital signs (height, weight, BMI, BP, HR, respiratory rate, O2, temperature, pediatric percentiles) |\n| PTCASE | 91 | Patient cases — the core clinical/billing unit (diagnosis codes ICD-9/ICD-10, insurance references, providers, co-pay, fee schedule, guarantor, billing sequence) |\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe export covers the USCDI/US Core clinical data classes reasonably well:\n\n**Clearly covered:**\n- Patient demographics (extensive — 80 columns including 25 custom fields)\n- Allergies (with standard coded terminologies)\n- Problems/conditions (SNOMED/ICD-10)\n- Medications (with RxNorm)\n- Lab results (extensive — 94 columns with specimen, facility, and provider details)\n- Vital signs (comprehensive PT-relevant vitals including pediatric percentiles)\n- Immunizations (very detailed — 52 columns)\n- Procedures (CPT-coded)\n- Goals\n- Family health history\n- Implantable devices (UDI data)\n- Program admissions/encounters (admission/discharge with coded disposition)\n- Some billing/case data via PTCASE (diagnosis codes, insurance references, co-pay, fee schedule, guarantor)\n\n**Notably missing or absent from documentation:**\n- **Clinical documentation / therapy notes** — This is the most significant gap. TheraOffice's core value proposition is clinical documentation for physical, occupational, and speech therapy. The product has 40+ customizable templates for evaluations, daily notes, progress notes, and treatment plans. None of these appear in the export tables. The documentation mentions that \"rich text documents or images\" are \"referenced in the extracts\" — but there is no table documenting how note content is exported, what format it takes, or what metadata accompanies it.\n- **Scheduling/appointments** — Not exported (though this is generally operational data, appointment data linked to encounters may be EHI)\n- **Claims and billing detail** — PTCASE has some billing metadata (insurance IDs, fee schedule type, diagnosis codes, billing sequence), but there are no tables for individual charges, CPT codes billed per visit, payment postings, ERA/remittance data, denial records, or collections data. TheraOffice is a fully integrated billing system — the billing detail is conspicuously absent.\n- **E-prescribing records** — The medications table captures active medications but there are no prescription/order tables with prescriber details, pharmacy, dispense instructions, refill history, or controlled substance tracking.\n- **Referral data** — PAT_PROFILE has REFERRALSOURCE_ID but there's no referral detail table (referring physician, authorization numbers, visit limits).\n- **Insurance/coverage detail** — PTCASE references INSURANCE1/2/3 as integer IDs but there's no insurance detail table with plan name, subscriber info, group numbers, policy dates.\n- **Documents and attachments** — No table for stored documents (scanned records, incoming faxes, uploaded files, correspondence).\n- **Telehealth visit records** — No mention despite TheraOffice supporting built-in telehealth.\n- **Patient portal communications** — No messaging or intake form submission data.\n- **Outcome measures and assessments** — PT practices extensively use standardized outcome measures (LEFS, DASH, NDI, Oswestry, etc.). These are not represented.\n- **Care plans and treatment plans** — Beyond the generic GOALS table, there's no structured treatment plan data.\n\n### The (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Assessment\n\nThe table naming convention is revealing: 16 of the 19 tables are prefixed with `PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_*`, and the export documentation is organized on a page titled \"EHI All Data Export.\" However, the data scope aligns almost perfectly with USCDI v1/v3 data classes rather than with \"all electronic health information.\"\n\nThis is a textbook example of the (b)(10)/(g)(10) conflation. The export appears to have been built by creating flat-file equivalents of the USCDI data classes that would also be exposed via the (g)(10) FHIR API — demographics, allergies, medications, problems, labs, vitals, immunizations, procedures, goals, implantable devices. The `PAT_PROFILE` and `PAT_PROFILE_CORE` tables add some additional demographic detail, and `PTCASE` adds case/billing metadata, but the overall shape is \"patient clinical summary\" not \"complete designated record set.\"\n\nFor a physical therapy EMR, the most critical clinical data is the therapy-specific documentation — evaluations, daily treatment notes, progress reports, discharge summaries, functional outcome measures, exercise programs. This is the data that directly drives care decisions, insurance authorization, and billing. Its absence from the export is a significant gap.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\n- **Format**: Delimited flat files (one file per table), with a SQL Server-derived schema (varchar, int, smalldatetime, etc.)\n- **Standard**: Proprietary/native format — not FHIR, C-CDA, or any recognized health data standard\n- **Encoding**: Not documented; presumably follows SQL Server defaults\n- **Delimiter**: Not specified in the documentation (described only as \"computable, delimited file format\")\n- **Relationships**: Tables are linked by `PAT_ID` (integer patient identifier). No explicit foreign key documentation. PTCASE has references to insurance and provider IDs but the lookup tables are not exported.\n- **Terminology**: Some SNOMED, ICD-10, and RxNorm codes are included inline. Value sets for coded fields (tinyint enums like ALLERGY_TYPE, REACTION, SEVERITY) are not documented.\n- **Reconstructability**: A third party could reconstruct basic patient demographics and clinical summaries. Without the reference/lookup tables for coded fields (insurance, providers, facilities, coded enums), the export would contain many integer IDs that are uninterpretable. The documentation says the tool \"will also generate documentation specific to that organization's environment\" — suggesting the actual export may include additional context not in this generic documentation.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n- **Readability**: The PDF is clean, well-structured, and easy to navigate with a table of contents.\n- **Field definitions**: Minimal. The glossary defines 5 common fields (Id, Pat_ID, MODIFIED_USER, MODIFIED_DATE, CREATED_USER, CREATED_DATE). All other fields have no descriptions — only name, data type, and max length.\n- **Value sets**: Not documented. Numerous tinyint/smallint fields are clearly coded values (ALLERGY_TYPE, REACTION, SEVERITY, STATUS, GENDER, RACE, etc.) but no value set mappings are provided.\n- **Examples**: None. No sample export files or worked examples.\n- **Import feasibility**: A developer could read the delimited files into a database, but without value set definitions, field descriptions, or relationship documentation, interpretation would require significant reverse engineering or access to the organization-specific documentation mentioned in the overview.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Column-level with data types and max lengths — the minimum for a data dictionary.\n- **Descriptions**: Absent for individual columns (only 5 glossary entries for common fields).\n- **Value sets**: Absent.\n- **Relationships**: Implicit via naming convention (PAT_ID) but not formally documented.\n- **Versioning**: Dated (May 29, 2024) but no change history.\n\n## Narrative Assessment\n\nNetsmart's TheraOffice EHI export documentation represents a minimal-effort compliance approach. The positive aspects are real: the export uses native delimited files (not just a FHIR repackaging), there's a dedicated documentation page, the PDF is well-organized, and the data dictionary covers 19 tables with 526 columns.\n\nHowever, the export has two fundamental problems:\n\n**First, scope.** For a product whose entire purpose is physical therapy clinical documentation, the absence of therapy notes, evaluations, treatment plans, and outcome measures from the export is a critical gap. The `PAT_PROFILE_USCDI_*` naming convention reveals the approach: the vendor built the export around USCDI data classes rather than around the data their product actually stores. The PTCASE table adds some billing context, but it's a single-table summary, not the detailed billing/claims data that TheraOffice manages. This is a USCDI clinical summary repackaged as an \"all data\" export, not a genuine (b)(10) export of the designated record set.\n\n**Second, interpretability.** Even for the data that is exported, the documentation lacks the context needed to use it. Coded fields have no value set definitions. Integer foreign keys reference tables not included in the export. The delimiter character isn't specified. No examples are provided. The documentation says the export tool generates \"documentation specific to that organization's environment\" — which may fill some of these gaps at export time, but that organization-specific documentation is not publicly available for assessment.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL: https://www.ntst.com/lp/certifications/ehi-all-data-theraoffice\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (main certifications page links to product-specific EHI page)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- None significant. The page and downloads were straightforward and publicly accessible.\n- The ZIP contained Mac OS metadata files (`__MACOSX/`) which were ignored.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":578,"cache_read_input_tokens":92949,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":578},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"aeaf608a-2979-4777-bb7f-bdfc2f7eef6f","uuid":"54bc5117-650a-47ac-a23e-e2571da601e0"}
